{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_3","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc 'cat /logs/agent/skills/.system/skill-creator/SKILL.md'","aggregated_output":"---\nname: skill-creator\ndescription: Guide for creating effective skills. This skill should be used when users want to create a new skill (or update an existing skill) that extends Codex's capabilities with specialized knowledge, workflows, or tool integrations.\nmetadata:\n  short-description: Create or update a skill\n---\n\n# Skill Creator\n\nThis skill provides guidance for creating effective skills.\n\n## About Skills\n\nSkills are modular, self-contained packages that extend Codex's capabilities by providing\nspecialized knowledge, workflows, and tools. Think of them as \"onboarding guides\" for specific\ndomains or tasks—they transform Codex from a general-purpose agent into a specialized agent\nequipped with procedural knowledge that no model can fully possess.\n\n### What Skills Provide\n\n1. Specialized workflows - Multi-step procedures for specific domains\n2. Tool integrations - Instructions for working with specific file formats or APIs\n3. Domain expertise - Company-specific knowledge, schemas, business logic\n4. Bundled resources - Scripts, references, and assets for complex and repetitive tasks\n\n## Core Principles\n\n### Concise is Key\n\nThe context window is a public good. Skills share the context window with everything else Codex needs: system prompt, conversation history, other Skills' metadata, and the actual user request.\n\n**Default assumption: Codex is already very smart.** Only add context Codex doesn't already have. Challenge each piece of information: \"Does Codex really need this explanation?\" and \"Does this paragraph justify its token cost?\"\n\nPrefer concise examples over verbose explanations.\n\n### Set Appropriate Degrees of Freedom\n\nMatch the level of specificity to the task's fragility and variability:\n\n**High freedom (text-based instructions)**: Use when multiple approaches are valid, decisions depend on context, or heuristics guide the approach.\n\n**Medium freedom (pseudocode or scripts with parameters)**: Use when a preferred pattern exists, some variation is acceptable, or configuration affects behavior.\n\n**Low freedom (specific scripts, few parameters)**: Use when operations are fragile and error-prone, consistency is critical, or a specific sequence must be followed.\n\nThink of Codex as exploring a path: a narrow bridge with cliffs needs specific guardrails (low freedom), while an open field allows many routes (high freedom).\n\n### Anatomy of a Skill\n\nEvery skill consists of a required SKILL.md file and optional bundled resources:\n\n```\nskill-name/\n├── SKILL.md (required)\n│   ├── YAML frontmatter metadata (required)\n│   │   ├── name: (required)\n│   │   └── description: (required)\n│   └── Markdown instructions (required)\n└── Bundled Resources (optional)\n    ├── scripts/          - Executable code (Python/Bash/etc.)\n    ├── references/       - Documentation intended to be loaded into context as needed\n    └── assets/           - Files used in output (templates, icons, fonts, etc.)\n```\n\n#### SKILL.md (required)\n\nEvery SKILL.md consists of:\n\n- **Frontmatter** (YAML): Contains `name` and `description` fields. These are the only fields that Codex reads to determine when the skill gets used, thus it is very important to be clear and comprehensive in describing what the skill is, and when it should be used.\n- **Body** (Markdown): Instructions and guidance for using the skill. Only loaded AFTER the skill triggers (if at all).\n\n#### Bundled Resources (optional)\n\n##### Scripts (`scripts/`)\n\nExecutable code (Python/Bash/etc.) for tasks that require deterministic reliability or are repeatedly rewritten.\n\n- **When to include**: When the same code is being rewritten repeatedly or deterministic reliability is needed\n- **Example**: `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` for PDF rotation tasks\n- **Benefits**: Token efficient, deterministic, may be executed without loading into context\n- **Note**: Scripts may still need to be read by Codex for patching or environment-specific adjustments\n\n##### References (`references/`)\n\nDocumentation and reference material intended to be loaded as needed into context to inform Codex's process and thinking.\n\n- **When to include**: For documentation that Codex should reference while working\n- **Examples**: `references/finance.md` for financial schemas, `references/mnda.md` for company NDA template, `references/policies.md` for company policies, `references/api_docs.md` for API specifications\n- **Use cases**: Database schemas, API documentation, domain knowledge, company policies, detailed workflow guides\n- **Benefits**: Keeps SKILL.md lean, loaded only when Codex determines it's needed\n- **Best practice**: If files are large (>10k words), include grep search patterns in SKILL.md\n- **Avoid duplication**: Information should live in either SKILL.md or references files, not both. Prefer references files for detailed information unless it's truly core to the skill—this keeps SKILL.md lean while making information discoverable without hogging the context window. Keep only essential procedural instructions and workflow guidance in SKILL.md; move detailed reference material, schemas, and examples to references files.\n\n##### Assets (`assets/`)\n\nFiles not intended to be loaded into context, but rather used within the output Codex produces.\n\n- **When to include**: When the skill needs files that will be used in the final output\n- **Examples**: `assets/logo.png` for brand assets, `assets/slides.pptx` for PowerPoint templates, `assets/frontend-template/` for HTML/React boilerplate, `assets/font.ttf` for typography\n- **Use cases**: Templates, images, icons, boilerplate code, fonts, sample documents that get copied or modified\n- **Benefits**: Separates output resources from documentation, enables Codex to use files without loading them into context\n\n#### What to Not Include in a Skill\n\nA skill should only contain essential files that directly support its functionality. Do NOT create extraneous documentation or auxiliary files, including:\n\n- README.md\n- INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md\n- QUICK_REFERENCE.md\n- CHANGELOG.md\n- etc.\n\nThe skill should only contain the information needed for an AI agent to do the job at hand. It should not contain auxiliary context about the process that went into creating it, setup and testing procedures, user-facing documentation, etc. Creating additional documentation files just adds clutter and confusion.\n\n### Progressive Disclosure Design Principle\n\nSkills use a three-level loading system to manage context efficiently:\n\n1. **Metadata (name + description)** - Always in context (~100 words)\n2. **SKILL.md body** - When skill triggers (<5k words)\n3. **Bundled resources** - As needed by Codex (Unlimited because scripts can be executed without reading into context window)\n\n#### Progressive Disclosure Patterns\n\nKeep SKILL.md body to the essentials and under 500 lines to minimize context bloat. Split content into separate files when approaching this limit. When splitting out content into other files, it is very important to reference them from SKILL.md and describe clearly when to read them, to ensure the reader of the skill knows they exist and when to use them.\n\n**Key principle:** When a skill supports multiple variations, frameworks, or options, keep only the core workflow and selection guidance in SKILL.md. Move variant-specific details (patterns, examples, configuration) into separate reference files.\n\n**Pattern 1: High-level guide with references**\n\n```markdown\n# PDF Processing\n\n## Quick start\n\nExtract text with pdfplumber:\n[code example]\n\n## Advanced features\n\n- **Form filling**: See [FORMS.md](FORMS.md) for complete guide\n- **API reference**: See [REFERENCE.md](REFERENCE.md) for all methods\n- **Examples**: See [EXAMPLES.md](EXAMPLES.md) for common patterns\n```\n\nCodex loads FORMS.md, REFERENCE.md, or EXAMPLES.md only when needed.\n\n**Pattern 2: Domain-specific organization**\n\nFor Skills with multiple domains, organize content by domain to avoid loading irrelevant context:\n\n```\nbigquery-skill/\n├── SKILL.md (overview and navigation)\n└── reference/\n    ├── finance.md (revenue, billing metrics)\n    ├── sales.md (opportunities, pipeline)\n    ├── product.md (API usage, features)\n    └── marketing.md (campaigns, attribution)\n```\n\nWhen a user asks about sales metrics, Codex only reads sales.md.\n\nSimilarly, for skills supporting multiple frameworks or variants, organize by variant:\n\n```\ncloud-deploy/\n├── SKILL.md (workflow + provider selection)\n└── references/\n    ├── aws.md (AWS deployment patterns)\n    ├── gcp.md (GCP deployment patterns)\n    └── azure.md (Azure deployment patterns)\n```\n\nWhen the user chooses AWS, Codex only reads aws.md.\n\n**Pattern 3: Conditional details**\n\nShow basic content, link to advanced content:\n\n```markdown\n# DOCX Processing\n\n## Creating documents\n\nUse docx-js for new documents. See [DOCX-JS.md](DOCX-JS.md).\n\n## Editing documents\n\nFor simple edits, modify the XML directly.\n\n**For tracked changes**: See [REDLINING.md](REDLINING.md)\n**For OOXML details**: See [OOXML.md](OOXML.md)\n```\n\nCodex reads REDLINING.md or OOXML.md only when the user needs those features.\n\n**Important guidelines:**\n\n- **Avoid deeply nested references** - Keep references one level deep from SKILL.md. All reference files should link directly from SKILL.md.\n- **Structure longer reference files** - For files longer than 100 lines, include a table of contents at the top so Codex can see the full scope when previewing.\n\n## Skill Creation Process\n\nSkill creation involves these steps:\n\n1. Understand the skill with concrete examples\n2. Plan reusable skill contents (scripts, references, assets)\n3. Initialize the skill (run init_skill.py)\n4. Edit the skill (implement resources and write SKILL.md)\n5. Package the skill (run package_skill.py)\n6. Iterate based on real usage\n\nFollow these steps in order, skipping only if there is a clear reason why they are not applicable.\n\n### Skill Naming\n\n- Use lowercase letters, digits, and hyphens only; normalize user-provided titles to hyphen-case (e.g., \"Plan Mode\" -> `plan-mode`).\n- When generating names, generate a name under 64 characters (letters, digits, hyphens).\n- Prefer short, verb-led phrases that describe the action.\n- Namespace by tool when it improves clarity or triggering (e.g., `gh-address-comments`, `linear-address-issue`).\n- Name the skill folder exactly after the skill name.\n\n### Step 1: Understanding the Skill with Concrete Examples\n\nSkip this step only when the skill's usage patterns are already clearly understood. It remains valuable even when working with an existing skill.\n\nTo create an effective skill, clearly understand concrete examples of how the skill will be used. This understanding can come from either direct user examples or generated examples that are validated with user feedback.\n\nFor example, when building an image-editor skill, relevant questions include:\n\n- \"What functionality should the image-editor skill support? Editing, rotating, anything else?\"\n- \"Can you give some examples of how this skill would be used?\"\n- \"I can imagine users asking for things like 'Remove the red-eye from this image' or 'Rotate this image'. Are there other ways you imagine this skill being used?\"\n- \"What would a user say that should trigger this skill?\"\n\nTo avoid overwhelming users, avoid asking too many questions in a single message. Start with the most important questions and follow up as needed for better effectiveness.\n\nConclude this step when there is a clear sense of the functionality the skill should support.\n\n### Step 2: Planning the Reusable Skill Contents\n\nTo turn concrete examples into an effective skill, analyze each example by:\n\n1. Considering how to execute on the example from scratch\n2. Identifying what scripts, references, and assets would be helpful when executing these workflows repeatedly\n\nExample: When building a `pdf-editor` skill to handle queries like \"Help me rotate this PDF,\" the analysis shows:\n\n1. Rotating a PDF requires re-writing the same code each time\n2. A `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` script would be helpful to store in the skill\n\nExample: When designing a `frontend-webapp-builder` skill for queries like \"Build me a todo app\" or \"Build me a dashboard to track my steps,\" the analysis shows:\n\n1. Writing a frontend webapp requires the same boilerplate HTML/React each time\n2. An `assets/hello-world/` template containing the boilerplate HTML/React project files would be helpful to store in the skill\n\nExample: When building a `big-query` skill to handle queries like \"How many users have logged in today?\" the analysis shows:\n\n1. Querying BigQuery requires re-discovering the table schemas and relationships each time\n2. A `references/schema.md` file documenting the table schemas would be helpful to store in the skill\n\nTo establish the skill's contents, analyze each concrete example to create a list of the reusable resources to include: scripts, references, and assets.\n\n### Step 3: Initializing the Skill\n\nAt this point, it is time to actually create the skill.\n\nSkip this step only if the skill being developed already exists, and iteration or packaging is needed. In this case, continue to the next step.\n\nWhen creating a new skill from scratch, always run the `init_skill.py` script. The script conveniently generates a new template skill directory that automatically includes everything a skill requires, making the skill creation process much more efficient and reliable.\n\nUsage:\n\n```bash\nscripts/init_skill.py <skill-name> --path <output-directory> [--resources scripts,references,assets] [--examples]\n```\n\nExamples:\n\n```bash\nscripts/init_skill.py my-skill --path skills/public\nscripts/init_skill.py my-skill --path skills/public --resources scripts,references\nscripts/init_skill.py my-skill --path skills/public --resources scripts --examples\n```\n\nThe script:\n\n- Creates the skill directory at the specified path\n- Generates a SKILL.md template with proper frontmatter and TODO placeholders\n- Optionally creates resource directories based on `--resources`\n- Optionally adds example files when `--examples` is set\n\nAfter initialization, customize the SKILL.md and add resources as needed. If you used `--examples`, replace or delete placeholder files.\n\n### Step 4: Edit the Skill\n\nWhen editing the (newly-generated or existing) skill, remember that the skill is being created for another instance of Codex to use. Include information that would be beneficial and non-obvious to Codex. Consider what procedural knowledge, domain-specific details, or reusable assets would help another Codex instance execute these tasks more effectively.\n\n#### Learn Proven Design Patterns\n\nConsult these helpful guides based on your skill's needs:\n\n- **Multi-step processes**: See references/workflows.md for sequential workflows and conditional logic\n- **Specific output formats or quality standards**: See references/output-patterns.md for template and example patterns\n\nThese files contain established best practices for effective skill design.\n\n#### Start with Reusable Skill Contents\n\nTo begin implementation, start with the reusable resources identified above: `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/` files. Note that this step may require user input. For example, when implementing a `brand-guidelines` skill, the user may need to provide brand assets or templates to store in `assets/`, or documentation to store in `references/`.\n\nAdded scripts must be tested by actually running them to ensure there are no bugs and that the output matches what is expected. If there are many similar scripts, only a representative sample needs to be tested to ensure confidence that they all work while balancing time to completion.\n\nIf you used `--examples`, delete any placeholder files that are not needed for the skill. Only create resource directories that are actually required.\n\n#### Update SKILL.md\n\n**Writing Guidelines:** Always use imperative/infinitive form.\n\n##### Frontmatter\n\nWrite the YAML frontmatter with `name` and `description`:\n\n- `name`: The skill name\n- `description`: This is the primary triggering mechanism for your skill, and helps Codex understand when to use the skill.\n  - Include both what the Skill does and specific triggers/contexts for when to use it.\n  - Include all \"when to use\" information here - Not in the body. The body is only loaded after triggering, so \"When to Use This Skill\" sections in the body are not helpful to Codex.\n  - Example description for a `docx` skill: \"Comprehensive document creation, editing, and analysis with support for tracked changes, comments, formatting preservation, and text extraction. Use when Codex needs to work with professional documents (.docx files) for: (1) Creating new documents, (2) Modifying or editing content, (3) Working with tracked changes, (4) Adding comments, or any other document tasks\"\n\nEnsure the frontmatter is valid YAML. Keep `name` and `description` as single-line scalars. If either could be interpreted as YAML syntax, wrap it in quotes.\n\nDo not include any other fields in YAML frontmatter.\n\n##### Body\n\nWrite instructions for using the skill and its bundled resources.\n\n### Step 5: Packaging a Skill\n\nOnce development of the skill is complete, it must be packaged into a distributable .skill file that gets shared with the user. The packaging process automatically validates the skill first to ensure it meets all requirements:\n\n```bash\nscripts/package_skill.py <path/to/skill-folder>\n```\n\nOptional output directory specification:\n\n```bash\nscripts/package_skill.py <path/to/skill-folder> ./dist\n```\n\nThe packaging script will:\n\n1. **Validate** the skill automatically, checking:\n\n   - YAML frontmatter format and required fields\n   - Skill naming conventions and directory structure\n   - Description completeness and quality\n   - File organization and resource references\n\n2. **Package** the skill if validation passes, creating a .skill file named after the skill (e.g., `my-skill.skill`) that includes all files and maintains the proper directory structure for distribution. The .skill file is a zip file with a .skill extension.\n\nIf validation fails, the script will report the errors and exit without creating a package. Fix any validation errors and run the packaging command again.\n\n### Step 6: Iterate\n\nAfter testing the skill, users may request improvements. Often this happens right after using the skill, with fresh context of how the skill performed.\n\n**Iteration workflow:**\n\n1. Use the skill on real tasks\n2. Notice struggles or inefficiencies\n3. Identify how SKILL.md or bundled resources should be updated\n4. Implement changes and test again\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

<<'\"'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nprint(pd.__version__)\nPY\n\"'```\n\n## Patterns\n\n### 1) Standardize column names\n```python\nimport re\n\ndef normalize_cols(df):\n    df = df.copy()\n    df.columns = [re.sub(r\"'\"\\\\s+\\\", \\\" \\\", c).strip() for c in df.columns]\n    return df\n\"'```\n\n### 2) Trim whitespace + harmonize strings\n```python\nstr_cols = df.select_dtypes(include='\"'object').columns\nfor c in str_cols:\n    df[c] = df[c].astype(str).str.strip().replace({'': pd.NA, 'nan': pd.NA})\n\"'```\n\n### 3) Parse dates + numeric fields\n```python\n# Dates\nif '\"'Order Date' in df.columns:\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Numeric\nnum_cols = ['Quantity', 'Purchase Price Per Unit']\nfor c in num_cols:\n    if c in df.columns:\n        df[c] = pd.to_numeric(df[c], errors='coerce')\n\"'```\n\n### 4) Deduplicate rows\n```python\nbefore = len(df)\ndf = df.drop_duplicates()\nprint('\"'deduped', before - <<'\"'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nprint(pd.__version__)\nPY\n\"'```\n\n## Patterns\n\n### 1) Standardize column names\n```python\nimport re\n\ndef normalize_cols(df):\n    df = df.copy()\n    df.columns = [re.sub(r\"'\"\\\\s+\\\", \\\" \\\", c).strip() for c in df.columns]\n    return df\n\"'```\n\n### 2) Trim whitespace + harmonize strings\n```python\nstr_cols = df.select_dtypes(include='\"'object').columns\nfor c in str_cols:\n    df[c] = df[c].astype(str).str.strip().replace({'': pd.NA, 'nan': pd.NA})\n\"'```\n\n### 3) Parse dates + numeric fields\n```python\n# Dates\nif '\"'Order Date' in df.columns:\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Numeric\nnum_cols = ['Quantity', 'Purchase Price Per Unit']\nfor c in num_cols:\n    if c in df.columns:\n        df[c] = pd.to_numeric(df[c], errors='coerce')\n\"'```\n\n### 4) Deduplicate rows\n```python\nbefore = len(df)\ndf = df.drop_duplicates()\nprint('\"'deduped', before - len(df))\n\"'```\n\n### 5) Fix categorical consistency\n```python\n# Example: gender cleanup\nmap_gender = {\n    '\"'m':'Male', 'male':'Male', 'f':'Female', 'female':'Female',\n    'non-binary':'Non-binary', 'nonbinary':'Non-binary'\n}\nif 'Q-demos-gender' in df.columns:\n    df['Q-demos-gender'] = (\n        df['Q-demos-gender'].str.lower().map(map_gender).fillna(df['Q-demos-gender'])\n    )\n\"'```\n\n### 6) Drop impossible values\n```python\nif '\"'Quantity' in df.columns:\n    df.loc[df['Quantity'] <= 0, 'Quantity'] = pd.NA\nif 'Purchase Price Per Unit' in df.columns:\n    df.loc[df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] <= 0, 'Purchase Price Per Unit'] = pd.NA\n\"'```\n\n### 7) Save cleaned CSV\n```python\nout_path = '\"'/app/output/cleaned.csv'\ndf.to_csv(out_path, index=False)\n\"'```\n\n## Tips\n- Keep raw files untouched.\n- Log counts of removed/cleaned rows for traceability.\n- Normalize IDs and categories early to avoid merge failures.\nMD'","aggregated_output":"","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.started","item":{"id":"item_14","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"cat > /app/environment/skills/retail-timeseries-anomaly.md <<'MD'\n# retail-timeseries-anomaly\n\nPurpose: Build daily category time series, generate counterfactual predictions, and compute an anomaly index in [-100, 100].\n\n## Setup\n- Python 3.9+\n- \"'`pandas`, `numpy`, `statsmodels`\n\n```bash\npython - <<'\"'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np, statsmodels\nprint('ok')\nPY\n\"'```\n\n## Steps\n\n### 1) Aggregate daily spend\n```python\n# expected columns: Order Date, Category, Quantity, Purchase Price Per Unit\np['\"'date'] = pd.to_datetime(p['Order Date'], errors='coerce').dt.date\np['spend'] = p['Quantity'] * p['Purchase Price Per Unit']\ndaily = (p.groupby(['Category','date'], as_index=False)\n           .agg(total_spend=('spend','sum')))\n\"'```\n\n### 2) Fit counterfactual model per category\nUse pre-treatment history only. A simple linear + day-of-week model is robust and fast.\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom statsmodels.formula.api import ols\n\ndef fit_predict(df_cat, cutoff_date):\n    hist = df_cat[df_cat['\"'date'] < cutoff_date].copy()\n    hist['t'] = np.arange(len(hist))\n    hist['dow'] = pd.to_datetime(hist['date']).dt.dayofweek\n    # OLS: spend ~ t + dow (categorical)\n    model = ols('total_spend ~ t + C(dow)', data=hist).fit()\n\n    target = df_cat.copy()\n    target['t'] = np.arange(len(hist), len(hist) + len(target))\n    target['dow'] = pd.to_datetime(target['date']).dt.dayofweek\n    target['pred'] = model.predict(target)\n    return target\n\"'```\n\n### 3) Anomaly index per category\nIndex in [-100,100]. Clamp extreme deviations.\n\n```python\n# March 2020 window\nmarch = target[target['\"'date'].between('2020-03-01','2020-03-31')]\nactual = march['total_spend'].sum()\npred = march['pred'].sum()\nraw = 100 * (actual - pred) / (abs(pred) + 1e-9)\nindex = float(np.clip(raw, -100, 100))\n\"'```\n\n### 4) Rank surges and slumps\n```python\n# surges: highest index, slumps: lowest index\nsurges = anomaly.sort_values('\"'Anomaly_Index', ascending=False).head(10)\nslumps = anomaly.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=True).head(10)\n\"'```\n\n## Notes\n- If historical data is sparse, fall back to a simple mean by day-of-week.\n- Use winsorization on daily spend to reduce the impact of outliers.\nMD'","aggregated_output":"","exit_code":null,"status":"in_progress"}}

.agg(Total_Spend=('spend','sum')))\n\"'```\n\n### 3) Extensive margin: any purchase per user-category-period\n```python\nextensive = intensive.copy()\nextensive['\"'Has_Purchase'] = panel[['\"'Total_Spend','post',f]].dropna()\n    m = ols('Total_Spend ~ post + {} + post:{}'\n            .format(f,f), data=df).fit()\n    results.append({'feature': f, 'did_estimate': m.params.get('post:{}'\n                    .format(f), np.nan), 'p_value': m.pvalues.get('post:{}'\n                    .format(f), np.nan)})\n\"'```\n\n### Multivariate heterogeneous DiD (extensive)\nIf binary outcome, use linear probability model for interpretability:\n`Has_Purchase ~ post + features + post:features`.\n\n```python\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\nX = panel[['\"'post'] + features]\nX = sm.add_constant(X)\n# add interactions\nfor f in features:\n    X[f'post_{f}'] = panel['post'] * panel[f]\nmodel = sm.OLS(panel['Has_Purchase'], X, missing='drop').fit()\n\"'```\n\n## Reporting\n- Sort top 3 drivers by sign depending on surge/slump.\n- Include p-values from the interaction terms.\nMD'","aggregated_output":"","exit_code":null,"status":"in_progress"}}

{"type":"item.started","item":{"id":"item_35","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"python - <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nimport re\nfrom datetime import datetime\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\n\nOUTPUT_DIR = '/app/output'\nSURVEY_PATH = '/app/data/survey_dirty.csv'\nPURCHASE_PATH = '/app/data/amazon-purchases-2019-2020_dirty.csv'\n\n# ------------------\n# Helpers\n# ------------------\n\ndef clean_str_series(s):\n    return s.astype(str).str.strip().replace({'': pd.NA, 'nan': pd.NA, 'None': pd.NA})\n\ndef safe_upper_underscore(val):\n    if pd.isna(val):\n        return pd.NA\n    s = str(val).strip()\n    if s == '':\n        return pd.NA\n    s = re.sub(r'\\\\s+', '_', s)\n    return s.upper()\n\n# ------------------\n# Load and clean survey\n# ------------------\n\nsurvey = pd.read_csv(SURVEY_PATH)\n\n# Normalize column names (trim only)\nsurvey.columns = [c.strip() for c in survey.columns]\n\n# Trim string columns\nfor c in survey.select_dtypes(include='object').columns:\n    survey[c] = clean_str_series(survey[c])\n\n# Remove exact duplicates\nsurvey = survey.drop_duplicates()\n\n# Drop duplicate ResponseID (keep first)\nif 'Survey ResponseID' in survey.columns:\n    survey = survey.drop_duplicates(subset=['Survey ResponseID'], keep='first')\n\n# Standardize state casing\nif 'Q-demos-state' in survey.columns:\n    survey['Q-demos-state'] = survey['Q-demos-state'].str.title()\n\n# Clean amazon use columns to expected categories\nif 'Q-amazon-use-howmany' in survey.columns:\n    allowed = {'1 (just me\"'!)'\"','2','3','4+'}\n    survey.loc[~survey['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].isin(allowed), 'Q-amazon-use-howmany'] = pd.NA\n\nif 'Q-amazon-use-hh-size' in survey.columns:\n    allowed = {'1 (just me\"'!)'\"','2','3','4+'}\n    survey.loc[~survey['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].isin(allowed), 'Q-amazon-use-hh-size'] = pd.NA\n\n# Save cleaned survey\nsurvey.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/survey_cleaned.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Load and clean purchases\n# ------------------\n\np = pd.read_csv(PURCHASE_PATH)\n# Trim strings\nfor c in p.select_dtypes(include='object').columns:\n    p[c] = clean_str_series(p[c])\n\n# Parse date\np['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(p['Order Date'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Numeric conversion\np['Purchase Price Per Unit'] = pd.to_numeric(p['Purchase Price Per Unit'], errors='coerce')\np['Quantity'] = pd.to_numeric(p['Quantity'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Drop invalid numeric or date\np = p.dropna(subset=['Order Date','Purchase Price Per Unit','Quantity','Survey ResponseID'])\n\n# Drop non-positive\np = p[(p['Purchase Price Per Unit'] > 0) & (p['Quantity'] > 0)]\n\n# Standardize category & state\np['Category'] = p['Category'].apply(safe_upper_underscore)\np['Shipping Address State'] = p['Shipping Address State'].str.title()\n\n# Fill missing category\np['Category'] = p['Category'].fillna('UNKNOWN')\n\n# Deduplicate\np = p.drop_duplicates()\n\n# Save cleaned purchases\np.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Anomaly detection\n# ------------------\n\n# Only consider through March 2020\ncutoff_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-31')\ncutoff_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-01')\n\np_anom = p[p['Order Date'] <= cutoff_end].copy()\n\n# Daily spend per category\np_anom['date'] = p_anom['Order Date'].dt.date\np_anom['spend'] = p_anom['Purchase Price Per Unit'] * p_anom['Quantity']\n\ndaily = (p_anom.groupby(['Category','date'], as_index=False)\n              .agg(total_spend=('spend','sum')))\n\n# Full date range\nall_dates = pd.date_range(daily['date'].min(), cutoff_end, freq='D').date\n\nanomaly_rows = []\n\n# Precompute day-of-week for full dates\nfull_date_df = pd.DataFrame({'date': all_dates})\nfull_date_df['dow'] = pd.to_datetime(full_date_df['date']).dt.dayofweek\n\nfor cat, df_cat in daily.groupby('Category'):\n    df = full_date_df.merge(df_cat, on='date', how='left')\n    df['total_spend'] = df['total_spend'].fillna(0.0)\n\n    hist = df[df['date'] < cutoff_start.date()].copy()\n    march = df[(df['date'] >= cutoff_start.date()) & (df['date'] <= cutoff_end.date())].copy()\n\n    # fallback if too little history\n    pred_march = None\n    if len(hist) < 30 or hist['total_spend'].sum() == 0:\n        if len(hist) >= 7:\n            dow_means = hist.groupby('dow')['total_spend'].mean()\n            pred_march = march['dow'].map(dow_means).fillna(hist['total_spend'].mean()).values\n        else:\n            pred_march = np.full(len(march), hist['total_spend'].mean() if len(hist)>0 else 0.0)\n    else:\n        # OLS with trend + day-of-week\n        hist = hist.reset_index(drop=True)\n        hist['t'] = np.arange(len(hist))\n        X = pd.get_dummies(hist['dow'], prefix='dow', drop_first=True)\n        X['t'] = hist['t']\n        X = sm.add_constant(X)\n        y = hist['total_spend']\n        try:\n            model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit()\n            march = march.reset_index(drop=True)\n            march['t'] = np.arange(len(hist), len(hist) + len(march))\n            X_m = pd.get_dummies(march['dow'], prefix='dow', drop_first=True)\n            X_m = X_m.reindex(columns=X.columns.drop('const'), fill_value=0)\n            X_m = sm.add_constant(X_m, has_constant='add')\n            pred_march = model.predict(X_m).values\n        except Exception:\n            # fallback to mean by dow\n            dow_means = hist.groupby('dow')['total_spend'].mean()\n            pred_march = march['dow'].map(dow_means).fillna(hist['total_spend'].mean()).values\n\n    pred_march = np.clip(pred_march, 0, None)\n    actual = march['total_spend'].sum()\n    pred = float(np.sum(pred_march))\n\n    if pred < 1 and actual < 1:\n        idx = 0.0\n    else:\n        raw = 100 * (actual - pred) / (abs(pred) + 1e-9)\n        idx = float(np.clip(raw, -100, 100))\n\n    anomaly_rows.append({'Category': cat, 'Anomaly_Index': round(idx, 4)})\n\nanomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_rows).sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\nanomaly_df.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/category_anomaly_index.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Feature engineering\n# ------------------\n\nsurvey_fe = survey.copy()\n\n# Age mapping to midpoints\nage_map = {\n    '18 - 24 years': 21,\n    '25 - 34 years': 29.5,\n    '35 - 44 years': 39.5,\n    '45 - 54 years': 49.5,\n    '55 - 64 years': 59.5,\n    '65 and older': 70,\n}\nif 'Q-demos-age' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['age_mid'] = survey_fe['Q-demos-age'].map(age_map)\n\n# Income ordinal\nincome_map = {\n    'Less than \"'$25,000'\"': 1,\n    '\"'$25,000 - $49,999'\"': 2,\n    '\"'$50,000 - $74,999'\"': 3,\n    '\"'$75,000 - $99,999'\"': 4,\n    '\"'$100,000 - $149,999'\"': 5,\n    '\"'$150,000 or more'\"': 6,\n}\nif 'Q-demos-income' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['income_ord'] = survey_fe['Q-demos-income'].map(income_map)\n\n# Education ordinal\nedu_map = {\n    'Some high school or less': 1,\n    'High school diploma or GED': 2,\n    \\\"Bachelor's degree\\\": 3,\n    'Graduate or professional degree (MA, MS, MBA, PhD, JD, MD, DDS, etc)': 4,\n}\nif 'Q-demos-education' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['education_ord'] = survey_fe['Q-demos-education'].map(edu_map)\n\n# Amazon use numeric\ndef parse_first_int(val):\n    if pd.isna(val):\n        return np.nan\n    m = re.search(r'(\\\\d+)', str(val))\n    if not m:\n        return np.nan\n    num = int(m.group(1))\n    if num > 10:\n        return np.nan\n    return num\n\nif 'Q-amazon-use-howmany' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['amazon_use_howmany_num'] = survey_fe['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].apply(parse_first_int)\n\nif 'Q-amazon-use-hh-size' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['amazon_use_hh_size_num'] = survey_fe['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].apply(parse_first_int)\n\n# Amazon frequency ordinal\nfreq_map = {\n    'Less than 5 times per month': 1,\n    '5 - 10 times per month': 2,\n    'More than 10 times per month': 3,\n}\nif 'Q-amazon-use-how-oft' in survey_fe.columns:\n    survey_fe['amazon_use_freq_ord'] = survey_fe['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].map(freq_map)\n\n# Binary indicators for yes/no style\nbinary_cols = [\n    'Q-demos-hispanic',\n    'Q-substance-use-cigarettes',\n    'Q-substance-use-marijuana',\n    'Q-substance-use-alcohol',\n    'Q-personal-diabetes',\n    'Q-personal-wheelchair'\n]\n\n# Keep categorical as-is for one-hot; binary map for clean numeric\nfor c in binary_cols:\n    if c in survey_fe.columns:\n        survey_fe[c + '_yes'] = survey_fe[c].str.lower().isin({'yes','y','true','1'}).astype('float')\n\n# One-hot encode categorical features\ncat_cols = [\n    'Q-demos-gender',\n    'Q-demos-race',\n    'Q-demos-education',\n    'Q-demos-income',\n    'Q-sexual-orientation',\n    'Q-demos-state',\n    'Q-amazon-use-howmany',\n    'Q-amazon-use-hh-size',\n    'Q-amazon-use-how-oft',\n    'Q-substance-use-cigarettes',\n    'Q-substance-use-marijuana',\n    'Q-substance-use-alcohol',\n    'Q-personal-diabetes',\n    'Q-personal-wheelchair',\n]\n\ncat_cols = [c for c in cat_cols if c in survey_fe.columns]\n\n# Sanitize category values in one-hot to be ASCII safe\nsurvey_cat = survey_fe[cat_cols].copy()\nfor c in survey_cat.columns:\n    survey_cat[c] = survey_cat[c].astype(str).str.strip().replace({'nan': pd.NA})\n\none_hot = pd.get_dummies(survey_cat, prefix=cat_cols, dummy_na=True)\n\n# Numeric features\nnum_cols = [c for c in [\n    'age_mid',\n    'income_ord',\n    'education_ord',\n    'amazon_use_howmany_num',\n    'amazon_use_hh_size_num',\n    'amazon_use_freq_ord'\n] if c in survey_fe.columns]\n\nnum_df = survey_fe[num_cols]\n\n# Binary yes indicators\nbin_cols = [c for c in survey_fe.columns if c.endswith('_yes')]\n\nfeatures = pd.concat([survey_fe[['Survey ResponseID']], num_df, survey_fe[bin_cols], one_hot], axis=1)\n\n# Ensure numeric type\nfor c in features.columns:\n    if c == 'Survey ResponseID':\n        continue\n    features[c] = pd.to_numeric(features[c], errors='coerce')\n\nfeatures.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/survey_feature_engineered.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Prepare DID datasets\n# ------------------\n\n# Identify top categories\nsurge_top = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False).head(10)\nslump_top = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=True).head(10)\n\nselected_categories = list(pd.concat([surge_top, slump_top])['Category'].unique())\n\n# Filter purchases to selected categories and periods\np_sel = p.copy()\np_sel['date'] = p_sel['Order Date'].dt.date\np_sel['spend'] = p_sel['Purchase Price Per Unit'] * p_sel['Quantity']\n\nbaseline_start = datetime(2020,1,1).date()\nbaseline_end = datetime(2020,2,29).date()\ntreatment_start = datetime(2020,3,1).date()\ntreatment_end = datetime(2020,3,31).date()\n\np_sel = p_sel[p_sel['Category'].isin(selected_categories)].copy()\n\n# Assign period\np_sel['Period'] = np.where((p_sel['date'] >= treatment_start) & (p_sel['date'] <= treatment_end), 'treatment',\n                    np.where((p_sel['date'] >= baseline_start) & (p_sel['date'] <= baseline_end), 'baseline', pd.NA))\n\np_sel = p_sel.dropna(subset=['Period'])\n\n# Intensive aggregation\nintensive = (p_sel.groupby(['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'], as_index=False)\n                  .agg(Total_Spend=('spend','sum')))\n\nintensive.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv', index=False)\n\n# Extensive aggregation (full panel of users)\nusers = survey['Survey ResponseID'].unique()\nperiods = ['baseline','treatment']\n\npanel_index = pd.MultiIndex.from_product([users, selected_categories, periods], names=['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'])\nextensive = pd.DataFrame(index=panel_index).reset_index()\n\n# Merge in observed purchases\nobs = intensive.copy()\nobs['Has_Purchase'] = (obs['Total_Spend'] > 0).astype(int)\n\nextensive = extensive.merge(obs[['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period','Has_Purchase']],\n                            on=['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'], how='left')\n\nextensive['Has_Purchase'] = extensive['Has_Purchase'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\nextensive.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Causal analysis (DiD)\n# ------------------\n\n# Prepare features matrix\nfeat_df = features.copy()\nfeat_df = feat_df.set_index('Survey ResponseID')\n\n# Standardize numeric features for comparability\nfeature_cols = [c for c in feat_df.columns if c \"'!= '\"'Survey ResponseID']\n\n# Identify binary columns (0/1)\nstandardized = feat_df.copy()\nfor c in feature_cols:\n    series = standardized[c]\n    if series.dropna().nunique() <= 1:\n        continue\n    vals = series.dropna().unique()\n    if set(vals).issubset({0,1}):\n        # keep binary as-is\n        standardized[c] = series.fillna(0)\n    else:\n        # z-score\n        mean = series.mean()\n        std = series.std()\n        if std == 0 or pd.isna(std):\n            standardized[c] = series.fillna(0)\n        else:\n            standardized[c] = (series - mean) / std\n            standardized[c] = standardized[c].fillna(0)\n\n# For consistency\nstandardized = standardized.fillna(0)\n\n# Helper to compute DiD for intensive (univariate)\n\ndef did_univariate_intensive(cat, direction):\n    # direction: 'surge' or 'slump' to sort\n    # Filter to purchasers in baseline or treatment\n    df_cat = intensive[intensive['Category'] == cat].copy()\n    df_cat = df_cat[df_cat['Total_Spend'] > 0]\n    if df_cat.empty:\n        return []\n    df_cat['post'] = (df_cat['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df_cat = df_cat.merge(standardized, left_on='Survey ResponseID', right_index=True, how='left')\n\n    results = []\n    for f in standardized.columns:\n        if f not in df_cat.columns:\n            continue\n        # skip constant\n        if df_cat[f].nunique() <= 1:\n            continue\n        try:\n            X = df_cat[['post', f]].copy()\n            X['post_x'] = df_cat['post'] * df_cat[f]\n            X = sm.add_constant(X)\n            model = sm.OLS(df_cat['Total_Spend'], X).fit(cov_type='HC1')\n            coef = model.params.get('post_x', np.nan)\n            pval = model.pvalues.get('post_x', np.nan)\n            results.append({'feature': f, 'did_estimate': float(coef), 'p_value': float(pval), 'method': 'Univariate DiD'})\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n\n    if not results:\n        return []\n\n    # Sort based on direction\n    if direction == 'surge':\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n    return results[:3]\n\n# Helper to compute DiD for standardized.columns if df_cat[f].nunique() > 1]\n    if not valid_feats:\n        return []\n\n    # Build design matrix\n    X = df_cat[['post'] + valid_feats].copy()\n    for f in valid_feats:\n        X[f'post_{f}'] = df_cat['post'] * df_cat[f]\n\n    X = sm.add_constant(X, has_constant='add')\n    y = df_cat['Has_Purchase'].astype(float)\n\n    try:\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC1')\n    except Exception:\n        return []\n\n    results = []\n    for f in valid_feats:\n        key = f'post_{f}'\n        if key in model.params:\n            results.append({\n                'feature': f,\n                'did_estimate': float(model.params[key]),\n                'p_value': float(model.pvalues.get(key, np.nan)),\n                'method': 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n            })\n\n    if not results:\n        return []\n\n    if direction == 'surge':\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], treat_panel['Total_Spend'] = 0.0\n    treat_obs = cat_int[cat_int['Period'] == 'treatment'][['Survey ResponseID','Total_Spend']]\n    treat_panel = treat_panel.merge(treat_obs, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left', suffixes=('','_obs'))\n    treat_panel['Total_Spend'] = treat_panel['Total_Spend_obs'].fillna(0)\n    treat_panel = treat_panel.drop(columns=['Total_Spend_obs'])\n\n    n_purch_base = int((base_panel['Total_Spend'] > 0).sum())\n    n_purch_treat = int((treat_panel['Total_Spend'] > 0).sum())\n\n    baseline_avg = float(base_panel.loc[base_panel['Total_Spend'] > 0, 'Total_Spend'].mean()) if n_purch_base > 0 else 0.0\n    treatment_avg = float(treat_panel.loc[treat_panel['Total_Spend'] > 0, 'Total_Spend'].mean()) if n_purch_treat > 0 else 0.0\n\n 'n_purchasers_treatment': n_purch_treat,\n        'baseline_purchase_rate': round(n_purch_base / users_count, 4),\n        'treatment_purchase_rate': round(n_purch_treat / users_count, 4),\n        'n_at_risk': users_count,\n        'intensive_margin': intensive_drivers,\n        'extensive_margin': extensive_drivers,\n    })\n\nfor _, row in slump_top.iterrows():\n    cat = row['Category']\n treat_panel['Total_Spend'] = 0.0\n    treat_obs = cat_int[cat_int['Period'] == 'treatment'][['Survey ResponseID','Total_Spend']]\n    treat_panel = treat_panel.merge(treat_obs, on='Survey ResponseID', (df['date'] <= cutoff_end.date())].copy()\n\n    pred_march = None\n    if len(hist) < 30 or hist['total_spend'].sum() == 0:\n        if len(hist) >= 7:\n            dow_means = prefix='dow', drop_first=True)\n        X['t'] = hist['t']\n        X = sm.add_constant(X)\n        y = hist['total_spend']\n        try:\n            model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit()\n            march = march.reset_index(drop=True)\n            march['t'] = np.arange(len(hist), len(hist) + len(march))\n            X_m = pd.get_dummies(march['dow'], prefix='dow', drop_first=True)\n            X_m = X_m.reindex(columns=X.columns.drop('const'), fill_value=0)\n            X_m = sm.add_constant(X_m, has_constant='add')\n            pred_march = model.predict(X_m).values\n        except Exception:\n            dow_means = hist.groupby('dow')['total_spend'].mean()\n            pred_march = march['dow'].map(dow_means).fillna(hist['total_spend'].mean()).values\n\n    pred_march = np.clip(pred_march, 0, None)\n    actual = march['total_spend'].sum()\n    pred = float(np.sum(pred_march))\n\n    if pred < 1 and actual < 1:\n        idx = 0.0\n    else:\n        raw = 100 * (actual - pred) / (abs(pred) + 1e-9)\n        idx = float(np.clip(raw, -100, 100))\n\n    anomaly_rows.append({'Category': cat, 'Anomaly_Index': round(idx, 4)})\n\nanomaly_df = datetime(2020,3,1).date()\ntreatment_end = datetime(2020,3,31).date()\n\np_sel = p_sel[p_sel['Category'].isin(selected_categories)].copy()\n\np_sel['Period'] = np.where((p_sel['date'] >= treatment_start) & (p_sel['date'] <= treatment_end), 'treatment',\n                    np.where((p_sel['date'] >= baseline_start) & (p_sel['date'] <= baseline_end), 'baseline', pd.NA))\n\np_sel = p_sel.dropna(subset=['Period'])\n\nintensive = (p_sel.groupby(['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'], as_index=False)\n                  .agg(Total_Spend=('spend','sum')))\n\nintensive.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv', index=False)\n\nusers = survey['Survey ResponseID'].unique()\nperiods = ['baseline','treatment']\n\npanel_index = pd.MultiIndex.from_product([users, selected_categories, periods], names=['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'])\nextensive = pd.DataFrame(index=panel_index).reset_index()\n\nobs = intensive.copy()\nobs['Has_Purchase'] = (obs['Total_Spend'] > 0).astype(int)\n\nextensive = extensive.merge(obs[['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period','Has_Purchase']],\n                            on=['Survey ResponseID','Category','Period'], how='left')\n\nextensive['Has_Purchase'] = extensive['Has_Purchase'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\nextensive.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv', index=False)\n\n# ------------------\n# Causal analysis (DiD)\n# ------------------\n\nfeat_df = features.set_index('Survey ResponseID')\n\nfeature_cols = [c for c in feat_df.columns]\n\nstandardized = feat_df.copy()\nfor c in feature_cols:\n    series = standardized[c]\n    if series.dropna().nunique() <= 1:\n        standardized[c] = series.fillna(0)\n        continue\n    vals = series.dropna().unique()\n    if set(vals).issubset({0,1}):\n        standardized[c] = series.fillna(0)\n    else:\n        mean = series.mean(); std = series.std()\n        if std == 0 or pd.isna(std):\n            standardized[c] = series.fillna(0)\n        else:\n            standardized[c] = (series - mean) / std\n            standardized[c] = standardized[c].fillna(0)\n\nstandardized = standardized.fillna(0)\n\n\ndef did_univariate_intensive(cat, direction):\n    df_cat = intensive[intensive['Category'] == cat].copy()\n    df_cat = df_cat[df_cat['Total_Spend'] > 0]\n    if df_cat.empty:\n        return []\n    df_cat['post'] = (df_cat['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df_cat = df_cat.merge(standardized, left_on='Survey ResponseID', right_index=True, how='left')\n\n    results = []\n    for f in standardized.columns:\n        if f not * df_cat[f]\n            X = sm.add_constant(X)\n            X = X.astype(float)\n            model = sm.OLS(df_cat['Total_Spend'], X).fit(cov_type='HC1')\n            coef = model.params.get('post_x', np.nan)\n            pval = model.pvalues.get('post_x', np.nan)\n            results.append({'feature': f, 'did_estimate': float(coef), 'p_value': float(pval), 'method': 'Univariate DiD'})\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n\n    if not results:\n        return []\n\n    if direction == 'surge':\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n    return results[:3]\n\n\ndef did_multivariate_extensive(cat, direction):\n    df_cat = extensive[extensive['Category'] == cat].copy()\n    df_cat['post'] = (df_cat['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df_cat = df_cat.merge(standardized, left_on='Survey ResponseID', right_index=True, how='left')\n\n    valid_feats = [f for f in standardized.columns if df_cat[f].nunique() > 1]\n    if not valid_feats:\n        return []\n\n    base_X = df_cat[['post'] + valid_feats].copy()\n    # interactions in one shot to avoid fragmentation\n    interactions = df_cat[valid_feats].multiply(df_cat['post'], axis=0)\n    interactions.columns = [f'post_{c}' for c in valid_feats]\n\n    X = pd.concat([base_X, interactions], axis=1)\n    X = sm.add_constant(X, has_constant='add')\n    X = X.astype(float)\n\n    y = df_cat['Has_Purchase'].astype(float)\n\n    try:\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC1')\n    except Exception:\n        return []\n\n    results = []\n    for f in valid_feats:\n        key = f'post_{f}'\n        if key in model.params:\n            results.append({\n                'feature': f,\n                'did_estimate': float(model.params[key]),\n                'p_value': float(model.pvalues.get(key, np.nan)),\n                'method': 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n            })\n\n    if not results:\n        return []\n\n    if direction == 'surge':\n        results = sorted(results, key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], 'Total_Spend'].mean()) if n_purch_treat > 0 else 0.0\n\n    report['surge_categories'].append({\n        'category': cat,\n        'anomaly_index': idx,\n        'baseline_avg_spend':
